Maybe use "alternating peak shaving": Identify peak lag k*. To reduce c_k*, choose pairs i,j with i+j=k* that have large product a_i a_j. def natural_gradient_step(a, beta, max_eta=10.0, min_eta=1e-4):

    c = self_conv(a); m=c.max()

    corr = true_corr(a,p)
    mean = dot(a,corr)
    # direction log change d = -(corr-mean)
    # try eta candidates ascending/descending
    for eta in [maybe adapt]: a_try = a * exp(-eta*(corr-mean)); normalize; if score<best accept

Function:
```
def natural_step_lsearch(a, beta, n_etas=8):
   c=self_conv(a); m=c.max(); score=...
   p=softmax(beta*(c-m)); corr=true_corr(a,p); mean=dot(a,corr)
   d=corr-mean
   R=np.max(np.abs(d)); if R<eps return a,score
   for q in [0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0,5.0,10.0]:
       eta=q/R
       a_try=a*np.exp(-eta*d)
       a_try=np.maximum(a_try,0)
       S=a_try.sum(); if S<eps continue
       a_try/=S
       sc=compute_score(a_try)
       if sc<score: accept update eta? maybe continue with next q? Accept best q and break or continue until no.
   if no improve: maybe accept small q? For exploration, can accept if sc< best? else random pert.
```

```
def lp_alternating(a, deadline, max_outer, fista_iters, beta, line_search_candidates=None):
    n=len(a); a=normalize(a); best=a; best_sc=score; x_init=a; step=1.0
    for outer in range(max_outer):
       if time>deadline: break
       c=self_conv(a); M=c.max()
       x,p_last=fista_lp(a,M,beta,fista_iters,x_init,step=1.0)
       Sx=x.sum()
       if Sx<1e-12 or Sx<=0: break
       b=x/Sx
       # optional if cross max with a > M*1.5 scale? check
       # line search t: include 0? We want only accept improvements; if t=0 no improve. Try t=0.2..1.
       for t in [0.05,0.1,0.15,0.25,0.4,0.6,0.8,1.0]:
           a_try=(1-t)*a+t*b normalize
           sc=compute_score(a_try)
           if sc<best_sc: update best, a, x_init=a? maybe keep b? 
       if no improve, maybe try t=1 with randomized? or reduce beta? break after several
       x_init=b (or a)
```
Need decide `x_init`: For next outer, use b or previous x? If using b normalized, it is feasible? It may not satisfy Bb≤M_new. But LP objective can start anywhere. Previous uses x_warm. We can set x_init=b.copy() for continuity. Also maybe keep unnormalized x? Since objective max sum; scaling maybe. Use x (unnormalized) warm if sum positive. But after mixing, scale changes. Use x/Sx.

Let's implement a "Backtracking natural gradient" with many steps:
- Keep `eta_scale=1.0` (multiplier q). At each iter, d, R, try eta = eta_scale/R. If improves, accept and maybe increase eta_scale *=1.2 up to 10; if not, try eta_scale/=2 until improve; if none, perturb.

Pseudo:
```
def natural_gradient_polish(a, beta, time_limit, max_iters, rng):
    a=normalize; best=a; best_sc=score; eta_q=1.0
    for it in range(max_iters):
       c=self_conv(a); ... compute d,R
       improved=False
       for attempt in range(6):
           eta=eta_q/R
           a_try=exp(-eta*d) capped; normalize; sc=score
           if sc<best_sc:
               best_sc=sc; best=a_try; a=a_try; improved=True; eta_q=min(10,eta_q*1.2); break
           else:
               eta_q=max(0.02,eta_q*0.5)
       if not improved:
           if it%20==0: random perturb; recompute best
           else maybe beta increase
       if time>limit break
```

### Numerical helpers
```
def _pow2(m):
    if m <=1: return 1
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()

def normalize_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size ==0: return np.ones(64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-15:
        a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / a.size
    else:
        a = a / s
    return a
```
For self_conv:
```
def self_convolution(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    m=2*n-1
    L=_pow2(m)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    return np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:m]
```
For cross conv in fista:
```
def conv_fixed_a_x(a, x, fa=None, L=None):
    n=a.size
    if L is None: L=_pow2(2*n-1)
    if fa is None: fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    fx=np.fft.rfft(x,L)
    return np.fft.irfft(fa*fx,L)[:2*n-1]

def true_corr_fixed_a_p(a, p, fa=None, L=None):
    n=a.size
    if L is None: L=_pow2(2*n-1)
    if fa is None: fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
    return np.fft.irfft(np.conj(fa)*fp, L)[:n]
```
Stable softmax:
```
def stable_softmax(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    zm = np.max(z)
    if not np.isfinite(zm):
       return np.ones_like(z)/z.size
    ez=np.exp(z-zm)
    s=ez.sum()
    if s <=0 or not finite: return uniform
    return ez/s
```
Compute score:
```
def compute_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float); n=a.size
    S=a.sum()
    if S <=1e-12: return inf
    c=self_convolution(a/S) # score scale invariant, explicit normalize for stability
    m=float(c.max())
    return float(2.0*n*m)
```
Since normalized sum=1, score =2n*max. Do not need /S^2.
Note: If a has zeros and sum normalized, fine.

LSE value and p:
```
def lse_max_and_probs(c, beta):
    cm=float(np.max(c))
    z=beta*(c-cm)
    zmax=float(np.max(z)) # <=0
    ez=np.exp(z-zmax)
    s=ez.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite:
       p=np.zeros_like(c); idx=np.argmax(c); p[idx]=1; return cm, p
    p=ez/s
    logsum = zmax + np.log(s)
    L = cm + logsum/beta
    return L,p
```
But zmax may be exactly 0 if max achieved; if not due cm max yes zmax=0. Good.

FISTA LP:
```
def fista_lp_solve(a, M, beta, iters, x_init=None, step=1.0):
    n=a.size
    a=normalize_sequence(a)
    L=_pow2(2*n-1); fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    if x_init is None: x=a.copy() # feasible? max Bx=M, sum1
    else: x=np.maximum(np.asarray(x_init,dtype=float).ravel(),0.0); x.resize n
    y=x.copy(); t=1.0; p=None
    # clip x to reasonable? if sum >1e6 scale?
    s=sum x
    if s <1e-15: x=a.copy(); y=a.copy()
    for it in range(iters):
       c=np.fft.irfft(fa*np.fft.rfft(x,L),L)[:2*n-1]
       z=beta*(c-M)
       p=stable_softmax(z)
       rp=true_corr_fixed_a_p(a,p,fa,L)
       grad=rp - 1.0
       # Step maybe adapt if step too large? use safety
       y_try = y - step*grad
       x_new=np.maximum(y_try,0.0)
       t_new = (1.0 + np.sqrt(1.0 + 4.0*t*t))/2.0
       y = x_new + (t - 1.0)/t_new*(x_new - x)
       # Standard FISTA: y may be negative. We can optionally not clip.
       x = x_new
       t=t_new
       # To avoid huge y, if not finite break
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
           x = np.maximum(y,0); finite break
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(y)):
           y = x.copy()
    return x, p
```
But FISTA with y negative and then x_new = max(y - step grad,0). If y huge negative, x_new zeros maybe. Could clip y to, say, [-1e6,1e6]. Not necessary. However, clipping y to nonnegative is not standard but may improve. Which is better? For projection prox, FISTA algorithm:
 y^{k+1} = x^{k+1} + ((t_k-1)/t_{k+1})(x^{k+1}-x^k)
 x^{k+1}=prox( y^k - grad y^k)
with y initial x. In standard, y can leave prox domain; that's okay. Prox clips. If y has large negative, prox zero. Good. If y has large positive, x may huge causing objective? But gradient should control. We can add a `if not finite: break`.

Step: Use step=0.9 to be safe. If S=1, L≤1. Use 0.9.
But if a not normalized? we normalize.

Potential problem: The LP objective F has no strongly convex term; prox of linear gradient. FISTA convergence. Good.

What is optimum x scale? Could x sum become huge? If a has very small entries for some constraints, x variable can be huge? But Bx constraints M, variable b_j's contribution to all constraints through a_{k-j}. If a_i all ≤1, M~1e-4, each x_j maybe ≤M/a_min. a_min could be 1e-6, x_j≤100. Sum up to 7000*100=700k. But likely not; if a_min extremely small, constraints involving small a relax, x may blow. To ensure bounded, we can enforce lower bound floor in a for LP: `a_lp = np.maximum(a, 1e-8); normalize`. Then x_j≤M/1e-8=10 if M=1e-4. Good. Use for LP only; for other methods use a. Also in fista, if x sum >1e5, scale? We can cap x to e.g. 10? But capping may bias. Use floor 1e-8 ensures.
Maybe a_min can be truly optimal <1e-8? But 1e-8 biases negligible? Values around 1e-4 usually. Fine.

`lp_alternating` function:
```
def lp_alternating(a, deadline, beta_schedule, fista_iters_schedule, max_total_outer, step=0.9):
   a=normalize(a)
   best=a; best_sc=compute_score(a)
   x_warm=a.copy()
   no=0
   for beta,fits in zip(...) maybe schedule repeated.
      for outer in range(...):
        if time>deadline: return
        a_lp = np.maximum(a, 1e-8); a_lp/=sum
        M = float(self_convolution(a_lp).max())
        x, p = fista_lp_solve(a_lp, M, beta, fits, x_warm, step)
        Sx=x.sum()
        if Sx<1e-12: break
        b=x/Sx
        # line search t candidates
        candidates = [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0]
        best_t=0; best_t_sc=best_sc
        for t in candidates:
            a_try=(1-t)*a + t*b
            a_try=normalize(a_try)
            sc=compute_score(a_try)
            if sc < best_t_sc:
                best_t_sc=sc; best_t=t
        if best_t>0 and best_t_sc < best_sc:
            a = normalize((1-best_t)*a + best_t*b)
            best = a.copy(); best_sc=best_t_sc; no=0
            x_warm = b.copy()
        else:
            no +=1
            if no>=8:
                # maybe try t=1 with perturb? or break to next beta
                # Could still set a = b if score not too much? no.
                # Add small random perturb then continue
                a = normalize(a + 0.001*rng.normal?) no rng param? pass
                if no>=20: break
   return best,best_sc
```

### Natural gradient polish
```
def natural_gradient_polish(a, beta, max_iters, time_limit, rng, use_line_search=True):
    a=normalize(a); best=a; best_sc=compute_score(a)
    eta_q=1.0
    for it in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic()>time_limit: break
        c=self_convolution(a)
        sc=2*n*c.max()
        p=stable_softmax(beta*(c-c.max()))  # use lse helper for p
        corr=true_corr_fixed_a_p(a,p)
        mean=float(np.dot(a,corr))
        d=corr-mean
        R=float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
        if R < 1e-15:
            break
        improved=False
        # Try current eta and reduce
        for _ in range(5):
            eta=eta_q/R
            # cap exponent
            ex=np.clip(-eta*d, -50.0, 50.0)
            a_try=a*np.exp(ex)
            a_try=np.maximum(a_try,0.0)
            s=a_try.sum()
            if s<1e-15:
                eta_q*=0.5; continue
            a_try/=s
            sc_try=compute_score(a_try)
            if sc_try < best_sc:
                best_sc=sc_try; best=a_try; a=a_try
                eta_q=min(10.0, eta_q*1.15)
                improved=True
                break
            else:
                eta_q=max(0.02, eta_q*0.5)
        if not improved:
            # random kick
            if it % 10 == 0:
                strength = 0.01 * max(a)
                a = normalize(a * (1.0 + strength*rng.standard_normal(n)))
                best = a.copy(); best_sc=compute_score(a)
            # if stuck long, break
    return best,best_sc
```
But this uses a lot of score evals: each failed attempt 2 FFT. 5 attempts. Could be okay if steps moderate. We can run e.g., 2000 steps =10k eval ~50s. For n=7000. Good.

We can optimize by reusing `c` for current? After update, need new c. For attempts, each candidate requires score. Could at low speed.

Maybe use a LSE score for acceptance to avoid exact? Exact not much slower (~one FFT convolution). Candidate score: self_conv one FFT. Fine.

What if natural gradient increases some peaks; accept only exact decrease. It may stagnate; random kick.

Can use top K weighting: Instead of p=softmax(beta*(c-m)), if β too high p one-hot; direction d=active lag a_{k-j}. That coordinate subgradient. Could be okay. We can schedule β high.

Add momentum? Maybe skip.

### L-BFGS θ
```
def theta_objective(x, beta):
    n = len(x)+1
    theta = np.empty(n); theta[0]=0.0; theta[1:]=x
    zm=theta.max(); e=np.exp(theta-zm); s=e.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: return 1e100, np.zeros_like(x)
    a=e/s
    c=self_convolution(a)
    L,p=lse_max_and_probs(c,beta)
    f=2.0*n*L
    corr=np.fft... true_corr
    mean=float(np.dot(a,corr))
    grad_full=4.0*n*a*(corr-mean)
    g=grad_full[1:]
    g=np.clip(g, -1e6,1e6)
    return f,g
```
But `lse_max_and_probs` returns L,p; needs beta. Good.
In objective, `c.max()` inside lse; if c all zero? a positive sum1 so not.

Let's write top-level:
```
def direct_lse_f_and_g(x, beta, pen):
    x = np.asarray(x,dtype=float).ravel()
    x = np.clip(x,0.0,1e6)
    S = x.sum()
    if S < 1e-12:
       x = np.ones_like(x)/x.size; S=1.0
    a = x/S
    n=a.size
    Lc=_pow2(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,Lc)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,Lc)[:2*n-1]
    Lval,p=lse_max_and_probs(c,beta)
    f=2.0*n*Lval + pen*(S-1.0)*(S-1.0)
    corr=np.fft.irfft(np.conj(fa)*np.fft.rfft(p,Lc),Lc)[:n]
    mean=float(np.dot(a,corr))
    g_score=(4.0*n*(corr-mean))/S
    g = g_score + 2.0*pen*(S-1.0)
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    return f, g
```
But note: `lse_max_and_probs` computes Lval and p. It also uses c.max and beta. Good.
The `pen` scale: If S deviates 0.01, penalty pen*1e-4. Score ~1.5. If pen=1000, penalty 0.1; okay. Use pen=1000.
Bounds: [(0,100.0)]*n. L-BFGS-B needs list; for n large memory okay (~n tuples). Could be 100k too large; for n >20000 maybe avoid direct LBFGS or use theta? We'll only run for n ≤20000. For large, natural/LP.

def run_lbfgs_direct(a0,beta,maxiter,maxfun):
    a0=normalize(a0); n=a0.size

Let's think if there are more powerful mathematical algorithms.

Could formulate as "minimax load balancing" on a path graph? c_k = sum over edges (i,k-i). Think of placing mass on vertices, loads on sums. We can use subgradient method with Bregman. Natural gradient is one. But maybe using "cutting plane"? We can maintain a set of active constraints lags and solve a convex relaxation if we linearize c_k around current a? At current a, c_k linearization: c_k(a+d) ≈ c_k(a) + ∇c_k(a)·d. Minimize maximum of these linear approximations over simplex and small step? This is a linear program (SPO+). We can update a to minimize first-order upper bound of max? Since c_k nonconvex, linearization not global. But local? We can solve LP:
minimize max_k [c_k(a) + (∇c_k(a))·d] s.t. a+d≥0, sum=1, maybe d bounded. ∇c_k(a)_j = 2a_{k-j}. This is linear in d. Could use FISTA? The LP has 2n-1 constraints, n variables. But maybe active set small; not.

Alternatively, use "Frank-Wolfe" on convex hull of subgradients? We want minimize F(a)=max_k c_k. The directional derivative at a for direction d is max_{k active} ∇c_k·d. Frank-Wolfe: choose s = argmin_{simplex} ∇F? Since F nonconvex, maybe. `s` has one coordinate 1 (if subgradient min coordinate). Then move a towards e_i. This is coordinate move decreasing most subgradient? Maybe too coarse. Natural gradient better.

`build_initializations`: Need rng. Top-level. Use no uniform_filter1d to avoid import? Could use simple moving average via FFT? Not necessary. For random smooth, use `np.fft.irfft(np.abs(np.fft.rfft(noise))**?`? Simpler: generate random low-freq coefficients and ifft.
```
def build_initializations(n, rng, parent):
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   inits=[]
   if parent is not None and len(parent)>1:
      inits.append(resample_sequence(parent,n))
   inits.append(np.ones(n))
   inits.append(0.2+0.8*(1.0-x))
   inits.append(0.2+0.8*x)
   for p in [0.3,0.7,1.0,2.0]:
      inits.append(0.1+0.9*np.clip((1.0-x)**p,0,1))
      inits.append(0.1+0.9*np.clip(x**p,0,1))
   # phase sinusoids
   for phase in [-0.8,-0.4,0.0,0.4,0.8]:
      inits.append(np.clip(0.7+0.3*np.cos(2*np.pi*x+phase),0.01,1.0))
   # random smooth
   for _ in range(3):
       spec=rng.standard_normal(1+n//2)
       spec=np.abs(spec)**2 # nonnegative? random
       sig=np.fft.irfft(spec, n)[:n]
       sig=sig-sig.min()+0.1
       inits.append(sig)
   # two-sided power singular finite
   eps=1e-3
   inits.append(0.2+0.8/(np.sqrt(x+eps)+np.sqrt(1-x+eps)+1.0))
   # maybe anti-symmetric step
   inits.append(np.clip(0.2+0.3*(1-x)+0.5*(x<0.6),0.01,1.0))
   # normalize all
   out=[]
   for arr in inits:
       arr=normalize_sequence(arr)
       if np.isfinite(arr).all(): out.append(arr)
   return out
```
No nested.

### Natural gradient details
`natural_gradient_polish` should use `a` normalized. At each iteration compute c, p, corr. Score exact computed. Use `best_sc` for acceptance, not current? If current already best. We can let current be best always. If an attempt improves best, update. If no attempt, perturb maybe. But if perturb makes score worse than best, keep best as a? We need maintain a as best to avoid drift. For perturb, we can create candidate `a_pert`, if score < best_sc update; else keep a. Since we want only improvements. But to escape local, we must accept worse? The task needs final best; temporary worse may help but can spend time. Could use simulated annealing: accept worse with probability but track best. However, deterministic accepted steps only may stagnate. Maybe allow small random restarts from best. For exploration, in main we generate diverse initializations and polish; within polish, only improvements. Good.

`natural_gradient_polish` can at no improve for many iterations do a random restart from `best` with strong perturb, but still only update if improvement; a for subsequent steps can be perturbed even if temporary worse? To escape, yes. We can set `a = a_pert` (maybe worse) but keep `best` separate; after some iterations if no improvement, return best. This can find new basins. Implement:
- `a = best.copy()`
- maintain `stagnation=0`
- if no improvement in a step, stagnation++
- if stagnation > 20: perturb `a` (not best) with strength 0.05*max(a); recompute; if score improves best update and a=best; else reset stagnation=0 and continue from perturbed worse.
- If perturbed score much worse and after X, reset a=best.
This allows escape.

But line search within step may fail for perturbed worse because `best_sc` high threshold; if we only accept improvements over best, no progress from perturbed. We need local search accept improvements relative to current `a_score`, not global best, to move from perturbed. So maintain `cur_sc`. If attempt < cur_sc, update a and cur_sc; if cur_sc < best_sc, update best. If stagnation current no improve from cur, perturb. This is typical.

Implement:
```

def refine_sequence(a, deadline, rng, do_lp=True, do_lbfgs=True):
    a=normalize(a); best=...;
    # smooth first natural low beta with 25% of remaining based on initial remaining
    t0=time.monotonic(); rem0=deadline-t0
    d1=min(deadline, t0 + 0.15*rem0 if rem0>50 else deadline)
    a,sc = natural_gradient_polish(a, 50.0, d1, rng, max_iters=5000)
    # LP with 40%
    if do_lp:
       d2=min(deadline, t0+0.55*rem0 if rem0>100 else deadline)
       a,sc = lp_alternating(a, d2, rng)
    # LBFGS 25%
    if do_lbfgs and a.size <= 20000:
       d3=min(deadline, t0+0.80*rem0)
       a,sc = lbfgs_polish(a, d3, rng)
    # final natural high to deadline
    a,sc = natural_gradient_polish(a, 3000.0, deadline, rng, max_iters=20000)
    return a,sc
```
But if rem0 small, fractions may overlap; using t0 fractions okay. If rem0=100, d1=t0+15, d2=t0+55, d3=t0+80, final to deadline. Good.

def lbfgs_polish(a, deadline, rng):
    a=normalize; best=a; best_sc=score; cur=a; cur_sc=best_sc
    betas=[100,400,1200,4000]

Let's flesh each.

#### pow2
```
def _pow2(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()
```
Input int.

#### normalize_sequence
Use `dtype=np.float64`. If size 0 return np.ones(64)/64. For NaN, replace 0. Upper clip? For internal, leave finite positive. Maybe clip to 1e12 to avoid overflow? We can `a = np.clip(a,0,1e12)`. Then sum. If sum finite and >0.
```
def normalize_sequence(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size ==0:
       return np.ones(64,dtype=np.float64)/64.0
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    a=np.minimum(a,1e12)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
       return np.full(a.size, 1.0/a.size)
    return a/s
```

#### self_convolution
```
def self_convolution(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.zeros(0)
    m=2*n-1
    L=_pow2(m)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,L)[:m]
    # numerical tiny negative? convolution of nonneg should nonneg, but FFT errors maybe -1e-17
    # max unaffected.
    return c
```

#### true_corr_fixed
```
def true_corr_fixed(a,p,fa=None,L=None):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if L is None: L=_pow2(2*n-1)
    if fa is None: fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
    return np.fft.irfft(np.conj(fa)*fp,L)[:n]
```
Ensure p length maybe 2n-1; if shorter, rfft pads zeros. Good.

#### lse_max_probs
```
def lse_max_probs(c,beta):
    c=np.asarray(c,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if c.size==0: return 0.0, np.zeros(0)
    cm=float(np.max(c))
    z=beta*(c-cm)
    zm=float(np.max(z))
    # zm should <=0 but if cm not? due float, maybe.
    ez=np.exp(z - zm)
    s=float(ez.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
       p=np.zeros(c.size,dtype=np.float64)
       idx=int(np.argmax(c)); p[idx]=1.0
       return cm,p
    p=ez/s
    logsum=zm + np.log(s)
    Lval=cm + logsum/beta
    return float(Lval),p
```
If beta<=0? We always >0.

#### compute_score
```
def compute_score(a):
    a=normalize_sequence(a)
    n=a.size
    c=self_convolution(a)
    m=float(np.max(c))
    return float(2.0*n*m)
```
Scale invariant. If n=0? no.

#### perturb_sequence
```
def perturb_sequence(a, rng, strength=0.05):
    a=normalize_sequence(a)
    n=a.size
    maxa=float(np.max(a)) if n else 1.0
    if maxa <=0: maxa=1.0
    noise=rng.standard_normal(n)
    # Multiplicative positive perturbation and additive
    a_try=a*(1.0 + strength*maxa*noise)
    a_try=np.maximum(a_try,0.0)
    return normalize_sequence(a_try)
```
Maybe strength relative to maxa; noise std 1. If maxa=0.01, strength 0.05 => relative 0.0005 too small? Actually a* (1+0.05*0.01*noise) changes 0.05% of value. Maybe we want absolute noise: `a + strength*maxa*noise`. That changes by 0.05*maxa=0.0005, relative interior 5%. Multiplicative relative changes small for interior? If a_i=1e-4, additive 0.0005 huge relative. Could be too strong. Use `scale = strength * np.mean(a)`? For n=7000 mean=1e-4; strength 0.1 => 1e-5, relative 10%. Good. Let's do additive:
```
base=float(np.mean(a))
a_try = a + strength*base*rng.standard_normal(n)
# also multiplicative small
a_try = np.maximum(a + strength*base*noise, 1e-12)
```
But if base small, okay. For escape, strength 0.3. We'll pass.

#### fista_lp_solve
Need pass `time_limit` optional. Use `a` normalized but for floor? We'll floor inside maybe parameter `floor=1e-8`.
```
def fista_lp_solve(a, M, beta, iters, x_init=None, time_limit=None, step=0.95, floor=1e-8):
    a0=normalize_sequence(a)
    a=np.maximum(a0, floor)
    a=a/a.sum()
    n=a.size
    L=_pow2(2*n-1); fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    if x_init is None:
       x=a.copy()
    else:
       x=np.asarray(x_init,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
       if x.size != n: x=np.resize(x,n)
       x=np.where(np.isfinite(x),x,0.0)
       x=np.maximum(x,0.0)
       # scale x to reasonable: if sum too large, cap? If sum > 1e5, scale down to 1e5? But LP optimum may >1e5 improbable. We can scale to 100 to be safe? Scaling down changes objective but warm okay.
       s=float(x.sum())
       if s <=1e-15:
           x=a.copy()
       else:
           # Do not force sum 1; but to prevent blow-up, if s>1e4 scale? Maybe not.
           if s > 1e4:
               x = x * (1.0/s)  # set sum1? Possibly loses warm. But safe.
    y=x.copy(); t=1.0; p=None
    for it in range(iters):
        if time_limit is not None and time.monotonic() > time_limit:
            break
        fx=np.fft.rfft(x,L)
        c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fx,L)[:2*n-1]
        z=beta*(c-M)
        p=stable_softmax(z)
        rp=true_corr_fixed(a,p,fa,L)
        grad=rp - 1.0
        y_try = y - step*grad
        # To avoid extreme negative y leading all zero, clip y_try to [-1000,1000]? If x warm sum1, grad range [-1,2]? step 1 => y_try maybe [-1,?]. Not huge. But FISTA momentum may. Clip y_try to [-100.0,100.0] maybe safe. But if optimum x100? floor ensures x≤100? M/floor=0.01? with M=1e-4 floor1e-8 => x≤10000. Clip 100 might cut. Use no clip or [-1e3,1e3].
        x_new=np.maximum(y_try,0.0)
        x_new=np.minimum(x_new,1e6) # finite
        t_new=(1+np.sqrt(1+4*t*t))/2
        y = x_new + (t-1)/t_new*(x_new-x)
        # clip y to finite
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(y)):
            y=x_new.copy()
        x=x_new; t=t_new
    return x,p
```
Should we use `a` original for true_corr? We use floored. Good.
Potential `p` from last iteration. If iters 0? not.

Stable_softmax top-level defined. Add.
```
def stable_softmax(z):
   z=np.asarray(z,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
   if z.size==0: return np.zeros(0)
   zm=float(np.max(z))
   if not np.isfinite(zm): return uniform
   ez=np.exp(z-zm)
   s=ez.sum()
   if s<=0 or not finite: uniform
   return ez/s
```

#### lp_alternating

def lp_alternating(a, deadline, rng=None, max_beta_schedules=None):
    a=normalize_sequence(a); best=a.copy(); best_sc=compute_score(a)
    if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng(12345)
    x_init=a.copy()
    schedules = [(20.0,1500),(50.0,2500),(100.0,3000),(300.0,3500),(800.0,3000),(2000.0,2500),(5000.0,2000)]
    t_list = (0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.75,1.0)
    global_no=0
    for beta,iters in schedules:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       beta_no=0
       while time.monotonic() < deadline:
           # compute M from a (or floor?) use normalize
           c=self_convolution(a); M=float(c.max())
           x, p = fista_lp_solve(a, M, beta, iters, x_init, time_limit=deadline, step=0.95)
           sx=float(x.sum())
           if sx <= 1e-15: break
           b=x/sx
           cur_sc=compute_score(a)
           best_t=0.0; best_t_sc=cur_sc
           for tt in t_list:
               a_try=np.maximum((1.0-tt)*a + tt*b,0.0)
               a_try=normalize_sequence(a_try)
               sc_try=compute_score(a_try)
               if sc_try < best_t_sc - 1e-14:
                   best_t_sc=sc_try; best_t=tt
           if best_t > 0.0 and best_t_sc < cur_sc:
               a=normalize_sequence(np.maximum((1.0-best_t)*a + best_t*b,0.0))
               cur_sc=best_t_sc
               if cur_sc < best_sc:
                   best_sc=cur_sc; best=a.copy()
               x_init=b.copy()
               global_no=0; beta_no=0
           else:
               beta_no +=1
               global_no +=1
               x_init=b.copy()
               if beta_no >= 4 or global_no >= 20:
                   # try small perturbation to escape but keep best
                   a_try=perturb_sequence(a, rng, strength=0.1)
                   sc_try=compute_score(a_try)
                   if sc_try < cur_sc:
                       a=a_try; cur_sc=sc_try
                       if cur_sc<best_sc: best=...;
                   # continue to next beta maybe
                   break
       # after schedule, if no improvements in whole, break? continue higher beta maybe
    return best,best_sc
```

def natural_gradient_polish(a, beta, max_iters, time_limit, rng=None, eta_start=1.0, perturb_strength=0.2):

#### direct_lse_f_and_g
Use self_conv_and_fa to avoid duplicate fa.
```
def direct_lse_f_and_g(x,beta,pen):
   x=np.asarray(x,dtype=float).ravel()
   x=np.clip(x,0.0,1e6)
   n=x.size
   S=float(x.sum())
   if S<1e-12:
      x=np.ones(n)/n; S=1.0
   a=x/S
   c,fa,L=self_convolution_and_fa(a)
   Lval,p=lse_max_probs(c,beta)
   f=2.0*n*Lval + pen*(S-1.0)*(S-1.0)
   fp=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
   corr=np.fft.irfft(np.conj(fa)*fp,L)[:n]
   mean=float(np.dot(a,corr))
   g=(4.0*n*(corr - mean))/S
   g=g + 2.0*pen*(S-1.0)
   g=np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
   # Maybe reduce scale: if n large, g huge? 4n*corr~ |corr|*n; corr~1e-4, n10000=>4; ok.
   return float(f), g
```
If x lower bound 0, gradient may be negative; L-BFGS respects bound.

#### estimate_eval_time
```
def estimate_direct_eval_time(a, beta, pen=100.0):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float).copy()
    t=time.monotonic()
    direct_lse_f_and_g(a,beta,pen)
    dt=time.monotonic()-t
    return dt if dt>1e-4 else 1e-4
```

def refine_sequence(a, deadline, rng, do_lp=True, do_lbfgs=True):
    a=normalize_sequence(a)
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=compute_score(a)
    t0=time.monotonic(); rem=max(0.0, deadline-t0)
    if rem <= 5: return best,best_sc
    # Stage 1: natural low beta 20%
    stage1 = min(deadline, t0 + 0.15*rem)
    if stage1 > t0 + 10:
        a,sc = natural_gradient_polish(a, 80.0, 8000, stage1, rng, eta_start=1.0, perturb_strength=0.3)
        if sc < best_sc: best=a.copy(); best_sc=sc
        else: a=best.copy()  # avoid worse? If natural may return best, which is <= input; okay.
    # Stage 2: LP 35%
    if do_lp:
       stage2 = min(deadline, t0 + 0.5*rem)
       if stage2 > t0+10:
          a,sc = lp_alternating(a, stage2, rng)
          if sc < best_sc: best=a.copy(); best_sc=sc
          else: a=best.copy()
    # Stage 3 LBFGS 25%
    if do_lbfgs and len(a) <= 20000:
       stage3 = min(deadline, t0 + 0.75*rem)
       if stage3 > t0+20:
          a,sc = lbfgs_polish(a, stage3, rng)
          if sc < best_sc: best=a.copy(); best_sc=sc
          else: a=best.copy()
    # final natural to deadline with high beta
    a,sc = natural_gradient_polish(a, 3000.0, 20000, deadline, rng, eta_start=2.0, perturb_strength=0.5)
    if sc < best_sc: best=a.copy(); best_sc=sc
    return best,best_sc

#### resample_sequence
```
def resample_sequence(a,n):
    a=normalize_sequence(a)
    if n<=0: n=1
    if len(a)==n: return a.copy()
    xl=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,len(a))
    xn=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    out=np.interp(xn,xl,a)
    return normalize_sequence(out)
```

#### build_initializations
Need no nested. Use `normalize_sequence`.
```
def build_initializations(n, rng, parent):
    inits=[]
    if parent is not None and len(parent)>0:
       inits.append(resample_sequence(parent,n))
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    inits.append(np.ones(n))
    for p in (0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0):
       inits.append(np.clip(0.05+0.95*(1.0-x)**p,0.01,10.0))
       inits.append(np.clip(0.05+0.95*(x)**p,0.01,10.0))
    for ph in (-1.2,-0.6,0.0,0.6,1.2):
       inits.append(np.clip(0.75+0.25*np.cos(2*np.pi*x+ph),0.01,10.0))
       inits.append(np.clip(0.75+0.25*np.sin(2*np.pi*x+ph),0.01,10.0))
    # endpoint singular finite
    eps=1e-3
    inits.append(np.clip(0.1+0.9/(np.sqrt(x+eps)+np.sqrt(1.0-x+eps)+1.0),0.01,10.0))
    inits.append(np.clip(0.1+0.9/(np.sqrt(x*x+eps)+np.sqrt((1-x)*(1-x)+eps)+1.0),0.01,10.0))
    # random smooth
    for _ in range(4):
       spec = rng.standard_normal(1+n//2)
       spec = np.abs(spec) ** 2
       # Force zero at high? not needed
       sig=np.fft.irfft(spec, n)[:n]
       sig=np.maximum(sig,0.0)
       if sig.max()>0: inits.append(sig)
    # two-block skew
    for cut in (0.4,0.5,0.6):
       arr=np.where(x<cut, 1.0, 0.2)
       inits.append(arr)
    out=[]
    for arr in inits:
       try:
          norm=normalize_sequence(arr)
          if np.all(np.isfinite(norm)):
             out.append(norm)
       except Exception:
          pass
    if len(out)==0:
       out.append(normalize_sequence(np.ones(n)))
    return out
```
Could be many; for quick, we may not use all.

#### choose_explore_lengths
```
def choose_explore_lengths(parent_len):
    cands=[6962,8192,10240,12288,16384,20480]
    if parent_len is not None:
       cands.insert(0,int(parent_len))
    seen=set(); out=[]
    for n in cands:
       if n>=256 and n<=25000 and n not in seen:
           out.append(n); seen.add(n)
       if len(out)>=5: break
    return out
```

       stage_dl = t0 + 0.45*(total_budget-30.0)
       print("Deep refine length")
       a,sc = refine_sequence(best_a, stage_dl, rng, do_lp=True, do_lbfgs=len(best_a)<=20000)
       if sc < best_sc:
          best_sc=sc; best_a=a.copy(); print
    # Explore other lengths
    explored=set(); if best_a: explored.add(len(best_a))
    explore_list=[n for n in lengths if n not in explored]
    # Maybe include one different initial not resampled for first length? We'll loop.
    for idx,n in enumerate(explore_list):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 120: break
       # allocate equal remaining among left + final
       left_count=len(explore_list)-idx
       remain=deadline-time.monotonic()
       alloc=min(300.0, max(60.0, remain/(left_count+1)*0.7))
       dl=time.monotonic()+alloc
       # initialization: if best_a available use resampled; else build init
       if best_a is not None and len(best_a)>0:
           init=resample_sequence(best_a,n)
           # maybe also random init if alloc>120? We'll just one resample for focus
       else:
           init=build_initializations(n,rng,None)[0]
       if n <= 12000:
           a,sc = refine_sequence(init, dl, rng, do_lp=True, do_lbfgs=True)
       else:
           a,sc = refine_sequence(init, dl, rng, do_lp=True, do_lbfgs=False)
       if sc < best_sc:
           best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=sc; print
    # Final deep polish if time
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 60 and best_a is not None:
       # maybe resample to best length or if best length not in lengths? no
       # choose if len best >20000 no lbfgs
       a,sc = refine_sequence(best_a, deadline-20, rng, do_lp=True, do_lbfgs=len(best_a)<=20000)
       if sc < best_sc: best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=sc
    # Final natural exact high with remaining
    if time.monotonic() < deadline-20 and best_a is not None:
       a,sc = natural_gradient_polish(best_a, 10000.0, 100000, deadline-10, rng, eta_start=2.0, perturb_strength=0.8)
       if sc < best_sc: best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=sc
    # fallback
    if best_a is None or len(best_a)==0:
       best_a=np.ones(64)/64; best_sc=2.0
    out = [float(max(0.0,min(1000.0,v))) for v in best_a]
    # check finite
    for i in range(len(out)):
       if not np.isfinite(out[i]): out[i]=0.0
    if len(out)==0 or sum(out)<0.01:
       out=[0.5]*64
    try:
       verified=evaluate_sequence(out)
    except Exception:
       verified=np.inf
    print final
    return out
```

Our L = maxc + (1/β)log∑exp(β(c_i-maxc)) = (1/β)log∑exp(βc_i) - maxc/β + maxc = (1/β)log∑exp(βc_i) + (1 -1/β) maxc. That's not standard smoothing! Wait! I made a mistake. The correct smooth approximation to max is `(1/β) log∑ exp(β c_i)`. Subtracting max for stability: L = (1/β)(max(βc)+log∑exp(β(c-max))) = max c + (1/β)log∑exp(β(c-max)). That equals standard LSE. Derivative: standard = ∑ p_i ∇c_i with p_i=exp(βc_i)/∑. Since subtract max in exponent doesn't change p. Derivative of maxc inside formula? This identity holds pointwise; derivative should be B^T p (not including derivative of cm separately because logsum term derivative compensates). Good. The p derivative from logsumshift: d/d a [ (1/β) log∑exp(β(c-cm))] = ∑ p_i ∇c_i - (∑p_i) ∇cm = B^T p - ∇cm. Plus dcm = ∇cm. Sum = B^T p. Good. Our gradient using `2*corr` = B^T p? ### More detailed `lp_alternating` code
Let's write fully:
```
def lp_alternating(a, deadline, rng):
    a=normalize_sequence(a)
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=compute_score(a); cur=a.copy(); cur_sc=best_sc
    x_init=a.copy()
    schedules = ((20.0,1500),(50.0,2500),(100.0,3000),(300.0,3500),(800.0,3000),(2000.0,2500),(5000.0,2000))
    t_list = (0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.75,1.0)
    global_no=0
    for beta,iters in schedules:
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       beta_no=0
       while time.monotonic() < deadline:
           c=self_convolution(a)
           M=float(c.max())
           if not np.isfinite(M) or M <=0: break
           x,p=fista_lp_solve(a,M,beta,iters,x_init,time_limit=deadline,step=1.0)
           sx=float(x.sum())
           if sx <=1e-15 or not np.isfinite(sx): break
           b=x/sx
           # line search
           best_t=0.0
           best_t_sc=cur_sc
           for tt in t_list:
              if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0: break
              a_try=np.maximum((1.0-tt)*a + tt*b,0.0)
              a_try=normalize_sequence(a_try)
              sc_try=compute_score(a_try)
              if sc_try < best_t_sc - 1e-14:
                  best_t_sc=sc_try; best_t=tt
           if best_t > 0.0:
              a=normalize_sequence(np.maximum((1.0-best_t)*a + best_t*b,0.0))
              cur_sc=best_t_sc
              if cur_sc < best_sc:
                  best_sc=cur_sc; best=a.copy()
              x_init=b.copy()
              global_no=0; beta_no=0
           else:
              beta_no +=1; global_no +=1
              x_init=b.copy()
              if beta_no >= 4 or global_no >= 30:
                  # perturb current a while keeping best
                  a_try=perturb_sequence(a, rng, strength=0.2)
                  sc_try=compute_score(a_try)
                  if sc_try < cur_sc:
                      a=a_try; cur_sc=sc_try
                      if cur_sc < best_sc:
                         best_sc=cur_sc; best=a.copy()
                  break
       # after schedule, reset cur to best if not improving? To avoid carrying local worse
       if best_sc < cur_sc:
          a=best.copy(); cur_sc=best_sc; x_init=a.copy()
    return best,best_sc
```
This is deterministic. If current a worse than best after perturb, we reset after beta. Good.

But if line search finds improvement smaller than current but current already worse than best, best may not update. After beta, reset to best. Good.

`fista_lp_solve` floor maybe modifies a. If current a has zeros, b from floor. fine.

def compute_score_normalized(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-15: return np.inf
    a=a/s
    c=self_convolution(a)
    return float(2.0*a.size*float(np.max(c)))
```

### Let's refine `natural_gradient_polish` code with helper `self_convolution_and_fa`
```
def natural_gradient_polish(a,beta,max_iters,time_limit,rng,eta_start=1.0,perturb_strength=0.3):
    a=normalize_sequence(a); best=a.copy(); best_sc=compute_score(a); cur_sc=best_sc
    eta=eta_start; stagn=0
    if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng(0)
    for it in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic()>time_limit: break
        c,fa,L=self_convolution_and_fa(a)
        m=float(np.max(c)); cur_sc_calc=float(2.0*a.size*m)
        if cur_sc_calc < cur_sc: cur_sc=cur_sc_calc # if previous?
        Lval,p=lse_max_probs(c,beta)
        corr=true_corr_fixed(a,p,fa,L)
        mean=float(np.dot(a,corr))
        d=corr-mean
        R=float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
        if R < 1e-14:
           break
        improved=False
        for _ in range(5):
            if time.monotonic()>time_limit: break
            eta_eff=eta/R
            ex=np.clip((-eta_eff*d).astype(np.float64), -50.0, 50.0)
            a_try=a*np.exp(ex)
            s=float(a_try.sum())
            if s <=1e-15:
                eta*=0.5
                continue
            a_try=a_try/s
            sc_try=compute_score(a_try)
            if sc_try < cur_sc - 1e-13:
                a=a_try; cur_sc=sc_try
                if cur_sc < best_sc:
                    best=a.copy(); best_sc=cur_sc
                eta=min(8.0, eta*1.25)
                improved=True
                break
            eta=max(0.02, eta*0.5)
        if improved:
            stagn=0
        else:
            stagn +=1
            if cur_sc > best_sc * 1.02 and it % 50 == 0:
                a=best.copy(); cur_sc=best_sc; eta=eta_start; stagn=0
            elif stagn >= 50:
                a=perturb_sequence(a,rng,strength=perturb_strength)
                cur_sc=compute_score(a)
                if cur_sc < best_sc:
                    best=a.copy(); best_sc=cur_sc
                eta=eta_start; stagn=0
    # Ensure return best, but if current better? best holds.
    return best,best_sc
```
`compute_score(a_try)` normalizes again; okay.
We recompute cur_sc from c each loop; if a is best and cur_sc older, okay. If perturb makes cur_worse, `cur_sc_calc` equals cur_sc. Good.

Could line search fail due comparing to cur_sc but cur_sc maybe equal best; okay.

### Direct LBFGS polish code with current/best
```
def lbfgs_polish(a,deadline,rng):
   a=normalize_sequence(a); best=a.copy(); best_sc=compute_score(a); cur=best.copy(); cur_sc=best_sc
   n=best.size
   if n<2 or n>25000: return best,best_sc
   bounds=[(0.0,100.0) for _ in range(n)]
   betas=(50.0,200.0,700.0,2000.0,6000.0)
   pen=100.0
   for beta in betas:
      if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
      t_eval=estimate_direct_eval_time(best,beta,pen)
      no_best=0
      for call in range(10):
         rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
         if rem < 20: break
         maxfun=int(min(6000, max(250, int(rem/(t_eval*5.0)))))
         if maxfun < 250: break
         res = minimize(direct_lse_f_and_g, cur, method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, args=(beta,pen), bounds=bounds, options={'ftol':1e-15,'gtol':1e-10,'maxiter':max(100,maxfun//4),'maxfun':maxfun,'maxls':30,'disp':False})
         a_out=normalize_sequence(res.x)
         sc=compute_score(a_out)
         if sc < best_sc:
             best=a_out.copy(); best_sc=sc; cur=a_out.copy(); cur_sc=sc; no_best=0
         else:
             no_best +=1
             if sc < cur_sc:
                 cur=a_out.copy(); cur_sc=sc
             else:
                 cur=best.copy(); cur_sc=best_sc
         if no_best >= 3:
             break
      cur=best.copy(); cur_sc=best_sc
   return best,best_sc
```
`estimate_direct_eval_time` may be expensive but okay. If t_eval too small, maxfun large but capped 6000. If t_eval 0.05, rem 100 => maxfun=400. Fine.

Potential: `minimize` may take longer than rem/(t_eval*5) if line search evaluates more than 5x maxfun? maxfun is total evaluations, each ~t_eval; time ~maxfun*t_eval + overhead. We set maxfun=rem/(5*t_eval), so ~20% of time. Good. But if SciPy overhead factor high, still safe. Using 5x.

### Numerical stability in `direct_lse_f_and_g`: `Lval` maybe `cm + logsum/beta`; for beta small, logsum ~log(2n) / beta maybe. cm ~1e-4. Score ~? okay. If beta=50, Lval - cm = logsum/50 ~0.1? Wait logsum~log(14000)=9.5, /50=0.19. That's huge compared to cm=1e-4! Did I make a scaling error: LSE `(1/β)log∑exp(β c_i)` when c_i ~1e-4 and n=7000. ```
def c_lnorm_weights(c, q):
    c=np.asarray... nonneg
    # if q <=0? not
    # use log c for positive; zeros -inf
    c_pos = c > 0
    if not any: p uniform? return 0,p
    logs = np.full(c.size -inf)
    logs[c_pos] = np.log(c[c_pos])
    z = q*logs
    zmax = np.max(z) # finite
    ez = np.exp(z - zmax)
    ez[~c_pos]=0
    s=ez.sum()
    if s<=0: p uniform/onehot max
    p=ez/s
    logsum = zmax + np.log(s)
    logN = logsum / q
    return logN, p
```

`compute Smooth`:
```
def smooth_norm_weights(c, q):
    c=np.asarray(c,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=c.size
    # ensure nonnegative
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    pos=c > 0
    if not np.any(pos):
       p=np.full(n,1.0/n); return 0.0,p
    if q <= 1.0:
       # l1? use uniform? q large always
    if q < 1e-6: q=1e-6
    logs=np.full(n,-np.inf)
    logs[pos]=np.log(c[pos])
    # if logs all -inf handled
    zmax=float(np.max(logs[pos])) # max log
    # For numerical, z = q*(logs - zmax) for pos, -inf for zeros
    ez=np.zeros(n)
    ez[pos]=np.exp(q*(logs[pos]-zmax))
    s=ez.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite:
       p=zeros; p[argmax]=1
       logN=zmax
    else:
       p=ez/s
       logN=zmax + np.log(s)/q
    return float(logN),p
```

```
def qnorm_weights_and_value(c, q):
    c=np.maximum(np.asarray(c,float).ravel(),0.0)
    K=c.size
    pos=c>0
    if not np.any(pos): return 0.0, np.full(K,1.0/K), 1.0
    logs=np.full(K,-np.inf); logs[pos]=np.log(c[pos])
    maxlog=float(logs[pos].max())
    # For Wq
    zq=np.zeros(K); zq[pos]=q*(logs[pos]-maxlog); ezq=np.exp(zq); szq=ezq.sum()
    if szq<=0: onehot max, logN=maxlog maybe? 
    logWq=maxlog + np.log(szq)
    logN=(logWq - np.log(K))/q
    # For W1 with exponent q-1
    exponent=q-1.0
    if exponent <=0: z1 zeros? q>1.
    z1=np.zeros(K); z1[pos]=exponent*(logs[pos]-maxlog)
    ez1=np.exp(z1); sz1=ez1.sum()
    logW1=maxlog + np.log(sz1)
    log_scalar=(1.0-q)*logN + logW1 - np.log(K)
    scalar=np.exp(log_scalar) if log_scalar > -745 else 0.0
    # tilde weights w1 normalized:
    # Use p1 = ez1 / sz1 (since exp((q-1)(logc-maxlog))) 
    p1=ez1/sz1
    Nval=np.exp(logN) if logN > -745 else 0.0
    return float(Nval), p1, float(scalar)
```

#### Check `qnorm_weights_and_value` for q<1? We'll pass q>1. But if q=100 okay.

```
def qnorm_weights_and_value(c, q):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    K = c.size
    if K == 0:
        return 0.0, np.zeros(0), 1.0
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    pos = c > 0.0
    if not np.any(pos):
        p = np.full(K, 1.0 / K)
        return 0.0, p, 1.0
    logs = np.full(K, -np.inf, dtype=np.float64)
    logs[pos] = np.log(c[pos])
    maxlog = float(np.max(logs[pos]))
    zq = np.zeros(K, dtype=np.float64)
    zq[pos] = q * (logs[pos] - maxlog)
    ezq = np.exp(zq)
    szq = float(ezq.sum())
    if szq <= 0 or not np.isfinite(szq):
       idx=int(np.argmax(c)); p=np.zeros(K); p[idx]=1.0
       return float(c[idx]), p, 1.0
    logWq = q * maxlog + np.log(szq)
    logN = (logWq - np.log(float(K))) / q
    exponent = q - 1.0
    if exponent < 0.0:
       exponent = 0.0
    z1 = np.zeros(K, dtype=np.float64)
    if exponent == 0.0:
       z1[pos] = 0.0
    else:
       z1[pos] = exponent * (logs[pos] - maxlog)
    ez1 = np.exp(z1)
    sz1 = float(ez1.sum())
    if sz1 <= 0 or not np.isfinite(sz1):
       p = np.zeros(K); p[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
       Nval=float(np.exp(logN)) if logN > -745 else 0.0
       return Nval, p, 1.0
    logW1 = exponent * maxlog + np.log(sz1)
    log_scalar = (1.0 - q) * logN + logW1 - np.log(float(K))
    if log_scalar > 700.0: scalar=1e300
    elif log_scalar < -745.0: scalar=0.0
    else: scalar=float(np.exp(log_scalar))
    p = ez1 / sz1
    if logN > 700.0: Nval=1e300
    elif logN < -745.0: Nval=0.0
    else: Nval=float(np.exp(logN))
    return Nval, p, scalar
```

If a feasible point with c just at M, small violations 1e-6 penalized β*1e-6=0.001 in log objective times? The -sum linear coeff 1. Balance. Larger β makes constraints tighter. Use up to 20000.
In FISTA, `z=beta*(c-M)`, c-M range maybe ±1e-4; beta=20000 -> z ±2; safe. If c-M range bigger 0.5? no.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative sequences minimizing 2*n*max(conv(a,a))/sum(a)^2.

Main ingredients:
- Correctly scaled FISTA for the LP alternating step (fixed convolution step size).
- Normalized high-order q-norm smoothing for direct local optimization, which avoids
  the large log-sum-exp offset and gives stable gradients near flat spectra.
- Natural-gradient / multiplicative local polish with backtracking and random escapes.
- Bounded L-BFGS-B polynomial refinement using the q-norm objective.
- Length exploration and final polishment under the 2400 s budget.
"""

import time

import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize


def _pow2(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64) / 64.0
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    a = np.minimum(a, 1e12)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def self_convolution(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    L = _pow2(m)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)[:m]
    return c


def self_convolution_and_fa(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    m = 2 * n - 1
    L = _pow2(m)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)[:m]
    return c, fa, L


def true_corr_fixed(a, p, fa=None, L=None):
    """
    Returns (B^T p)_j = sum_i a_i p_{i+j}, j=0..n-1,
    where p is a length-(2n-1) vector and B is the convolution matrix with fixed a.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if L is None:
        L = _pow2(2 * n - 1)
    if fa is None:
        fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    out = np.fft.irfft(np.conj(fa) * fp, L)[:n]
    return out


def stable_softmax(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = z.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    zm = float(np.max(z))
    if not np.isfinite(zm):
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    ez = np.exp(z - zm)
    s = float(ez.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        p = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        p[int(np.argmax(z))] = 1.0
        return p
    return ez / s


def qnorm_weights_and_value(c, q):
    """
    Smooth max-like objective based on the normalized q-norm:
        N_q = ((1/K) * sum(c_i^q))^(1/q)

    Returns:
      N_q,
      p_i proportional to c_i^(q-1) (weights for B^T),
      scalar such that dN_q/d a = 2 * scalar * B^T p.

    This avoids the log(N)/beta offset of plain log-sum-exp.
    """
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    K = c.size
    if K == 0:
        return 0.0, np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64), 1.0

    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    pos = c > 0.0
    if not np.any(pos):
        p = np.full(K, 1.0 / K, dtype=np.float64)
        return 0.0, p, 1.0

    maxc = float(np.max(c[pos]))
    maxlog = float(np.log(maxc))

    logs = np.full(K, -np.inf, dtype=np.float64)
    logs[pos] = np.log(c[pos])

    # Sum c_i^q / maxc^q
    zq = np.zeros(K, dtype=np.float64)
    zq[pos] = q * (logs[pos] - maxlog)
    ezq = np.exp(zq)
    szq = float(ezq.sum())
    if szq <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(szq):
        idx = int(np.argmax(c))
        p = np.zeros(K, dtype=np.float64)
        p[idx] = 1.0
        return float(c[idx]), p, 1.0

    # Sum c_i^(q-1) / maxc^(q-1)
    exponent = max(0.0, q - 1.0)
    z1 = np.zeros(K, dtype=np.float64)
    if exponent > 0.0:
        z1[pos] = exponent * (logs[pos] - maxlog)
    ez1 = np.exp(z1)
    sz1 = float(ez1.sum())
    if sz1 <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(sz1):
        idx = int(np.argmax(c))
        p = np.zeros(K, dtype=np.float64)
        p[idx] = 1.0
        ratio2 = szq / float(K)
        Nval = maxc * (ratio2 ** (1.0 / q))
        return float(Nval if np.isfinite(Nval) else maxc), p, 1.0

    Kf = float(K)
    ratio2 = szq / Kf
    ratio1 = sz1 / Kf

    Nval = maxc * (ratio2 ** (1.0 / q))
    scalar = ratio1 * (ratio2 ** ((1.0 - q) / q))

    if not np.isfinite(scalar) or scalar < 0.0:
        scalar = 1.0

    p = ez1 / sz1
    return float(Nval), p, float(scalar)


def compute_score(a):
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    c = self_convolution(a)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    return float(2.0 * n * m)


def perturb_sequence(a, rng, strength=0.3):
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a
    base = float(a.mean())
    if base <= 1e-300:
        base = 1.0 / n
    noise = rng.standard_normal(n)
    a_try = a + strength * base * noise
    a_try = np.maximum(a_try, 0.0)
    return normalize_sequence(a_try)


def fista_lp_solve(a, M, beta, iters, x_init=None, time_limit=None, step=1.0, floor=1e-8):
    """
    FISTA for convex surrogate:
        min_x  (1/beta)*logsumexp(beta*(conv(a,x)-M)) - sum(x), x >= 0
    which approximates max sum(x) s.t. conv(a,x) <= M, x >= 0.
    """
    a0 = normalize_sequence(a)
    a = np.maximum(a0, floor)
    a = a / a.sum()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64), np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    L = _pow2(2 * n - 1)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)

    if x_init is None:
        x = a.copy()
    else:
        x = np.asarray(x_init, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if x.size != n:
            x = np.resize(x, n)
        x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
        x = np.maximum(x, 0.0)
        sx = float(x.sum())
        if sx <= 1e-15:
            x = a.copy()
        elif sx > 1e6:
            x = x / sx

    y = x.copy()
    t = 1.0
    p = None

    for it in range(max(0, int(iters))):
        if time_limit is not None and time.monotonic() > time_limit:
            break

        fx = np.fft.rfft(x, L)
        c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fx, L)[:2 * n - 1]

        z = beta * (c - M)
        p = stable_softmax(z)

        rp = true_corr_fixed(a, p, fa, L)
        grad = rp - 1.0

        y_try = y - step * grad
        x_new = np.maximum(y_try, 0.0)
        x_new = np.minimum(x_new, 1e6)

        t_new = (1.0 + np.sqrt(1.0 + 4.0 * t * t)) / 2.0
        y = x_new + ((t - 1.0) / t_new) * (x_new - x)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(y)):
            y = x_new.copy()

        x = x_new
        t = t_new

        if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
            x = a.copy()
            y = x.copy()
            t = 1.0
            break

    if p is None:
        p = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
        if p.size > 0:
            p[0] = 1.0

    return x, p


def lp_alternating(a, deadline, rng=None):
    """
    Corrected LP-alternating local search.
    """
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    if rng is None:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(12345)

    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = compute_score(a)
    cur = a.copy()
    cur_sc = best_sc
    x_init = a.copy()

    schedules = (
        (50.0, 1500),
        (200.0, 2500),
        (1000.0, 3000),
        (5000.0, 3000),
        (20000.0, 2500),
    )
    t_list = (0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.35, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00)
    global_no = 0

    for beta, iters in schedules:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

        beta_no = 0
        while time.monotonic() < deadline:
            a_floor = np.maximum(cur, 1e-8)
            a_floor = a_floor / a_floor.sum()
            c = self_convolution(a_floor)
            M = float(np.max(c))
            if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
                break

            x, _ = fista_lp_solve(
                a_floor, M, beta, iters,
                x_init=x_init,
                time_limit=deadline,
                step=1.0
            )
            sx = float(x.sum())
            if sx <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(sx):
                break

            b = x / sx
            best_t = 0.0
            best_t_sc = cur_sc

            for tt in t_list:
                if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0:
                    break
                a_try = np.maximum((1.0 - tt) * cur + tt * b, 0.0)
                a_try = normalize_sequence(a_try)
                sc_try = compute_score(a_try)
                if sc_try < best_t_sc - 1e-14:
                    best_t_sc = sc_try
                    best_t = tt

            if best_t > 0.0:
                cur = normalize_sequence(np.maximum((1.0 - best_t) * cur + best_t * b, 0.0))
                cur_sc = best_t_sc
                if cur_sc < best_sc:
                    best_sc = cur_sc
                    best = cur.copy()
                x_init = b.copy()
                global_no = 0
                beta_no = 0
            else:
                beta_no += 1
                global_no += 1
                x_init = b.copy()
                if beta_no >= 4 or global_no >= 30:
                    a_try = perturb_sequence(cur, rng, strength=0.25)
                    sc_try = compute_score(a_try)
                    if sc_try < cur_sc:
                        cur = a_try
                        cur_sc = sc_try
                        if cur_sc < best_sc:
                            best_sc = cur_sc
                            best = cur.copy()
                    break

        if best_sc < cur_sc:
            cur = best.copy()
            cur_sc = best_sc
            x_init = best.copy()

    return best, best_sc


def natural_gradient_polish(a, q, max_iters, time_limit, rng=None,
                            eta_start=1.0, perturb_strength=0.35):
    """
    Multiplicative/natural-gradient local search for the normalized q-norm objective.
    """
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    if rng is None:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(98765)

    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = compute_score(a)
    cur = a.copy()
    cur_sc = best_sc

    eta = float(eta_start)
    stagn = 0
    n = a.size

    for it in range(max(0, int(max_iters))):
        if time.monotonic() > time_limit:
            break

        c, fa, L = self_convolution_and_fa(cur)
        _N, p, _scalar = qnorm_weights_and_value(c, q)
        corr = true_corr_fixed(cur, p, fa, L)
        mean = float(np.dot(cur, corr))
        d = corr - mean
        R = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))

        if R < 1e-14:
            break

        improved = False
        for _ in range(5):
            if time.monotonic() > time_limit:
                break

            eta_eff = eta / R
            ex = -eta_eff * d
            ex = np.clip(ex, -50.0, 50.0)
            a_try = cur * np.exp(ex)
            a_try = np.maximum(a_try, 0.0)
            s = float(a_try.sum())
            if s <= 1e-15:
                eta = max(0.02, 0.5 * eta)
                continue
            a_try = a_try / s
            sc_try = compute_score(a_try)

            if sc_try < cur_sc - 1e-13:
                cur = a_try
                cur_sc = sc_try
                if cur_sc < best_sc:
                    best_sc = cur_sc
                    best = cur.copy()
                eta = min(8.0, 1.25 * eta)
                improved = True
                break
            else:
                eta = max(0.02, 0.5 * eta)

        if improved:
            stagn = 0
        else:
            stagn += 1
            if cur_sc > best_sc * 1.02 and it % 50 == 0:
                cur = best.copy()
                cur_sc = best_sc
                eta = float(eta_start)
                stagn = 0
            elif stagn >= 50:
                cur = perturb_sequence(cur, rng, strength=perturb_strength)
                cur_sc = compute_score(cur)
                if cur_sc < best_sc:
                    best_sc = cur_sc
                    best = cur.copy()
                eta = float(eta_start)
                stagn = 0

        _ = n

    return best, best_sc


def direct_qnorm_f_and_g(x, q, pen):
    """
    Bounded L-BFGS-B objective + gradient for x >= 0.
    Uses normalized a = x/sum(x) and the normalized q-norm smooth max.
    """
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = x.size
    if n == 0:
        return 1e100, np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1e6)
    S = float(x.sum())
    if S <= 1e-15:
        x = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        S = 1.0

    a = x / S
    c, fa, L = self_convolution_and_fa(a)
    Nval, p, scalar = qnorm_weights_and_value(c, q)
    f = 2.0 * n * Nval + pen * (S - 1.0) * (S - 1.0)

    corr = true_corr_fixed(a, p, fa, L)
    grad_a = 4.0 * n * scalar * corr
    mean = float(np.dot(a, grad_a))
    g = (grad_a - mean) / S
    g = g + 2.0 * pen * (S - 1.0)
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    return float(f), g


def estimate_direct_eval_time(a, q, pen=1000.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    t = time.monotonic()
    direct_qnorm_f_and_g(a, q, pen)
    dt = time.monotonic() - t
    return max(1e-4, dt)


def lbfgs_polish(a, deadline, rng=None):
    """
    Bounded L-BFGS-B with progressive q-norm smoothing.
    """
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = a.size
    if n < 2 or n > 25000:
        return a, compute_score(a)

    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = compute_score(a)
    cur = a.copy()
    cur_sc = best_sc

    bounds = [(0.0, 100.0)] * n
    betas = (100.0, 1000.0, 10000.0, 100000.0)
    pen = 1000.0

    for q in betas:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

        t_eval = estimate_direct_eval_time(best, q, pen)
        no_best = 0

        for _call in range(10):
            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem < 20.0:
                break

            # Conservative maxfun based on measured eval time.
            maxfun = int(min(6000, max(250, int(rem / (t_eval * 5.0)))))
            if maxfun < 250:
                break

            maxiter = max(100, maxfun // 4)
            options = {
                "ftol": 1e-15,
                "gtol": 1e-10,
                "maxiter": maxiter,
                "maxfun": maxfun,
                "maxls": 30,
                "disp": False,
            }

            res = minimize(
                direct_qnorm_f_and_g,
                cur,
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                jac=True,
                args=(q, pen),
                bounds=bounds,
                options=options,
            )

            a_out = normalize_sequence(res.x)
            sc_out = compute_score(a_out)

            if sc_out < best_sc:
                best = a_out.copy()
                best_sc = sc_out
                cur = a_out.copy()
                cur_sc = sc_out
                no_best = 0
            else:
                no_best += 1
                if sc_out < cur_sc:
                    cur = a_out.copy()
                    cur_sc = sc_out
                else:
                    cur = best.copy()
                    cur_sc = best_sc

            if no_best >= 3:
                break

        cur = best.copy()
        cur_sc = best_sc

    return best, best_sc


def refine_sequence(a, deadline, rng, do_lp=True, do_lbfgs=True):
    """
    Multi-stage refinement with absolute deadline.
    """
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = compute_score(a)

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    rem = max(0.0, deadline - t0)
    if rem <= 5.0:
        return best, best_sc

    # 1) Smooth natural-gradient warm-up.
    stage1 = min(deadline, t0 + 0.15 * rem)
    if stage1 > t0 + 10.0:
        a, sc = natural_gradient_polish(a, 200.0, 8000, stage1, rng,
                                        eta_start=1.0, perturb_strength=0.35)
        if sc < best_sc:
            best = a.copy()
            best_sc = sc
        else:
            a = best.copy()

    # 2) Corrected LP alternating.
    if do_lp:
        stage2 = min(deadline, t0 + 0.50 * rem)
        if stage2 > t0 + 10.0:
            a, sc = lp_alternating(a, stage2, rng)
            if sc < best_sc:
                best = a.copy()
                best_sc = sc
            else:
                a = best.copy()

    # 3) L-BFGS polish with q-norm objectives (only for moderate size).
    if do_lbfgs and len(a) <= 20000:
        stage3 = min(deadline, t0 + 0.78 * rem)
        if stage3 > t0 + 20.0:
            a, sc = lbfgs_polish(a, stage3, rng)
            if sc < best_sc:
                best = a.copy()
                best_sc = sc
            else:
                a = best.copy()

    # 4) Final high-q natural gradient to deadline.
    a, sc = natural_gradient_polish(a, 100000.0, 50000, deadline, rng,
                                    eta_start=2.0, perturb_strength=0.6)
    if sc < best_sc:
        best = a.copy()
        best_sc = sc

    return best, best_sc


def resample_sequence(a, n):
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = max(1, int(n))
    if a.size == n:
        return a.copy()
    xl = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size)
    xn = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    out = np.interp(xn, xl, a)
    return normalize_sequence(out)


def build_initializations(n, rng, parent):
    inits = []
    n = max(32, int(n))

    if parent is not None and len(parent) > 1:
        inits.append(resample_sequence(parent, n))

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)

    inits.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    for p in (0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0):
        inits.append(np.clip(0.05 + 0.95 * np.power(1.0 - x, p), 0.01, 10.0))
        inits.append(np.clip(0.05 + 0.95 * np.power(x, p), 0.01, 10.0))

    for ph in (-1.2, -0.6, 0.0, 0.6, 1.2):
        inits.append(np.clip(0.75 + 0.25 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x + ph), 0.01, 10.0))
        inits.append(np.clip(0.75 + 0.25 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * x + ph), 0.01, 10.0))

    eps = 1e-3
    inits.append(np.clip(
        0.1 + 0.9 / (np.sqrt(x + eps) + np.sqrt(1.0 - x + eps) + 1.0),
        0.01, 10.0
    ))
    inits.append(np.clip(
        0.1 + 0.9 / (np.sqrt(x * x + eps) + np.sqrt((1.0 - x) * (1.0 - x) + eps) + 1.0),
        0.01, 10.0
    ))

    for _ in range(4):
        spec = rng.standard_normal(1 + n // 2)
        spec = np.abs(spec) ** 2
        sig = np.fft.irfft(spec, n)[:n]
        sig = np.maximum(sig, 0.0)
        if np.max(sig) > 0.0:
            inits.append(sig)

    for cut in (0.35, 0.5, 0.65):
        arr = np.where(x < cut, 1.0, 0.18)
        inits.append(arr)

    out = []
    for arr in inits:
        try:
            norm = normalize_sequence(arr)
            if np.all(np.isfinite(norm)):
                out.append(norm)
        except Exception:
            pass

    if len(out) == 0:
        out.append(normalize_sequence(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)))
    return out


def choose_explore_lengths(parent_len):
    cands = [6962, 8192, 10240, 12288, 16384]
    if parent_len is not None:
        try:
            cands.insert(0, int(parent_len))
        except Exception:
            pass

    seen = set()
    out = []
    for nn in cands:
        try:
            nn = int(nn)
        except Exception:
            continue
        if 512 <= nn <= 16384 and nn not in seen:
            out.append(nn)
            seen.add(nn)
        if len(out) >= 5:
            break

    if not out:
        out = [8192]
    return out


def propose_candidate(budget_s=2400.0):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = t0 + total_budget - 30.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(2024)

    best_a = None
    best_sc = np.inf

    parent = None
    try:
        h = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if h is not None:
            p = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if p.size >= 16 and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
                p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
                if p.sum() > 0:
                    parent = p
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None:
        parent = normalize_sequence(parent)
        best_a = parent.copy()
        best_sc = compute_score(parent)
        print(f"Parent length={parent.size}, internal score={best_sc:.8f}")

    lengths = choose_explore_lengths(len(best_a) if best_a is not None else None)

    if best_a is None:
        n0 = lengths[0]
        inits = build_initializations(n0, rng, None)
        for arr in inits[:12]:
            sc = compute_score(arr)
            if sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc
                best_a = arr.copy()
        print(f"Initial no-parent length={best_a.size}, score={best_sc:.8f}")

    # Deep refine current best (usually parent).
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 120.0 and best_a is not None:
        stage_dl = t0 + 0.55 * (total_budget - 30.0)
        print(f"Deep refining length={best_a.size}")
        a, sc = refine_sequence(best_a, stage_dl, rng,
                                do_lp=True,
                                do_lbfgs=(len(best_a) <= 20000))
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best_a = a.copy()
            print(f"  Deep refine score={best_sc:.8f}")

    # Explore a few nearby lengths by resampling the current best.
    explored = set()
    if best_a is not None and len(best_a) > 0:
        explored.add(len(best_a))
    explore_list = [nn for nn in lengths if nn not in explored]

    for idx, nn in enumerate(explore_list):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 150.0:
            break
        if best_a is None:
            break

        left_count = max(1, len(explore_list) - idx)
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        alloc = min(260.0, max(40.0, (rem / (left_count + 1.0)) * 0.8))
        dl = time.monotonic() + alloc
        if dl > deadline - 120.0:
            dl = deadline - 120.0
        if dl <= time.monotonic() + 10.0:
            continue

        init = resample_sequence(best_a, nn)
        print(f"Exploring length={nn}, allocation={alloc:.0f}s")
        a, sc = refine_sequence(init, dl, rng,
                                do_lp=True,
                                do_lbfgs=(nn <= 12000))
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best_a = a.copy()
            print(f"  New best: length={nn}, score={best_sc:.8f}")

    # Final deep polish of the best candidate.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 80.0 and best_a is not None:
        a, sc = refine_sequence(best_a, deadline - 25.0, rng,
                                do_lp=True,
                                do_lbfgs=(len(best_a) <= 16384))
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best_a = a.copy()
            print(f"Final refine score={best_sc:.8f}")

    # Last short high-q natural gradient push.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 20.0 and best_a is not None:
        a, sc = natural_gradient_polish(best_a, 200000.0, 50000, deadline - 12.0,
                                        rng, eta_start=2.5, perturb_strength=0.8)
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best_a = a.copy()
            print(f"Final natural score={best_sc:.8f}")

    if best_a is None or len(best_a) == 0:
        best_a = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64) / 64.0
        best_sc = 2.0

    out = [float(max(0.0, min(1000.0, v))) for v in np.asarray(best_a, dtype=np.float64)]
    for i in range(len(out)):
        if not np.isfinite(out[i]):
            out[i] = 0.0

    if len(out) == 0 or sum(out) < 0.01:
        out = [0.5] * 64

    try:
        verified = float(evaluate_sequence(out))
    except Exception:
        verified = np.inf

    elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
    print("=" * 70)
    print(f"FINAL internal={best_sc:.8f}, verified={verified:.8f}")
    print(f"FINAL length={len(out)}, time={elapsed:.1f}s / budget={total_budget:.0f}s")
    print("=" * 70)

    return out
```
